Avoid choosing answers based on outside knowledge. Passage 1: The Importance of Public Service Public service plays an important role in building a peaceful and organized society. Government employees are expected to perform their duties honestly, fairly, and efficiently. Their responsibility is not limited to completing assigned tasks; they must also ensure that public resources are used properly. When public officials prioritize personal interests over public welfare, trust in government institutions decreases. On the other hand, employees who demonstrate professionalism and accountability help strengthen public confidence. All government employees have the same responsibilities. Passage 2: Technology in Education Technology has changed the way students access information and learn new skills. Passage 15: The Importance of Accurate Information People make decisions every day based on information they receive from different sources. Passage 16: Responsible Citizenship Responsible citizenship involves understanding one's rights and fulfilling one's duties toward society. Citizens may also attend public consultations, express concerns through proper channels, and support initiatives that benefit their communities. Passage 17: Water Conservation Water is essential for human survival, agriculture, and economic activities. Despite its importance, many communities experience water shortages because of pollution, excessive consumption, and changing weather conditions. Conserving water does not always require expensive equipment or major lifestyle changes. Simple actions such as repairing leaking faucets, turning off taps when not in use, and collecting rainwater for suitable purposes can reduce unnecessary consumption. When individuals and communities practice conservation consistently, they help protect water resources for future generations. 81. What is the central message of the passage? A. Water conservation requires expensive technology. B. Water shortages occur only because of changing weather. C. Individuals cannot influence water consumption. D. Simple and consistent actions can help conserve water resources. 82. Which factor is mentioned as a cause of water shortages? A. Excessive water consumption. B. Proper maintenance of faucets. C. Responsible rainwater collection. D. Community conservation programs. 83. Why does the passage mention repairing leaking faucets? A. To explain how water can be made more expensive. B. To show that conservation requires professional assistance. C. To provide an example of a simple water-saving action. D. To suggest that all water systems should be replaced. 84. What can be inferred from the passage? A. Only government agencies can protect water resources. B. Small individual actions can contribute to larger conservation efforts. C. Water shortages can be completely prevented by repairing faucets. D. Collecting rainwater is suitable for every possible use. 85. What does the word "consumption" most nearly mean in the passage? A. Protection of natural resources. B. Production of agricultural goods. C. Distribution of water supplies. D. Use of water or other resources. Passage 18: Critical Thinking Critical thinking allows individuals to examine information carefully before reaching conclusions. Instead of accepting statements immediately, critical thinkers ask questions, consider available evidence, and examine different explanations. This skill is useful in both personal and professional situations. For example, an employee who encounters a workplace problem should identify its possible causes before choosing a solution. Making decisions without examining the facts may lead to mistakes or ineffective results. 86. What is the main idea of the passage? A. Critical thinking helps people evaluate information and make better decisions. B. People should avoid questioning information. C. Workplace problems can always be solved immediately. D. Personal opinions are more important than evidence. 87. According to the passage, what do critical thinkers do before reaching conclusions? A. Accept the first explanation they hear. B. Ignore information that challenges their opinions. C. Examine evidence and consider different explanations. D. Make decisions based on emotions alone. 88. To avoid accepting responsibility. D. To prove that other employees are responsible. 89. What can be inferred from the passage? A. Quick decisions are always more accurate. B. Evidence is unnecessary when a person has experience. C. Critical thinking is useful only in professional situations. D. Careful examination can reduce the risk of poor decisions. 90. What does the word "ineffective" most nearly mean? A. Not producing the intended result. B. Completely successful. C. Easy to understand. D. Supported by reliable evidence. Passage 19: Urban Development As cities grow, the demand for housing, transportation, and public services also increases. Urban development can create employment opportunities and improve access to essential facilities. However, rapid and poorly planned growth may result in overcrowding, pollution, and pressure on public infrastructure. Sustainable urban planning considers both present needs and future consequences. This includes developing efficient transportation systems, protecting green spaces, and ensuring that public facilities can serve growing populations. By considering these factors, communities can promote development without unnecessarily damaging the environment. 91. What is the main purpose of the passage? A. To argue that cities should stop growing. B. To explain why urban development should consider sustainability. C. To discuss only the economic benefits of urban development. D. To suggest that green spaces prevent employment opportunities. 92. Which is a possible negative effect of rapid and poorly planned urban growth? A. Improved public infrastructure in every area. B. Present needs and future consequences. B. Economic growth without environmental protection. C. The interests of property owners alone. D. Transportation systems without public facilities. 94. Passage 20: Personal Discipline Personal discipline helps individuals remain focused on their responsibilities even when they face difficulties or distractions. It involves managing one's actions, following commitments, and making decisions that support long-term goals. Motivation may encourage a person to begin a task, but motivation can change depending on circumstances. Discipline helps individuals continue working even when they do not feel motivated. By developing consistent habits and taking responsibility for their actions, people can make steady progress toward their objectives. 96. What is the main idea of the passage? A. Motivation is the only requirement for achieving goals. B. Personal discipline is unnecessary when tasks are difficult. C. People should wait until they feel motivated before working. D. Personal discipline helps people remain consistent and achieve long-term goals. 97. According to the passage, how does discipline differ from motivation? A. Discipline helps people continue working even when motivation decreases. B. Discipline depends entirely on receiving rewards. C. Motivation always remains constant. D. Motivation prevents people from facing difficulties. 98. Which behavior demonstrates personal discipline? A. Abandoning responsibilities whenever distractions appear. B. Waiting for other people to complete assigned tasks. C. Following commitments despite temporary difficulties. D. Changing goals whenever progress becomes challenging. 99. What can be inferred from the passage? A. People who lack motivation cannot accomplish anything. B. Consistent habits can help individuals make progress even during difficult periods. C. A. 180 B. 200 C. 220 D. 240 Decimals Batch 1 of 4 Questions 1–25 Quick lesson before the exam Think of money. ₱1.00 is one whole peso, while ₱0.50 is half a peso. - Addition/subtraction: Align the decimal points. - Multiplication: Multiply normally, then count decimal places. - Division: Move the decimal point to make the divisor a whole number. - Rounding: Look at the digit immediately after the place you're rounding to. A. 12 km/L B. 13 km/L C. 14 km/L D. 15 km/L Percentages Batch 1 of 4 Questions 1–25 Quick lesson: What does percentage mean? Percentage means a part out of 100. Imagine a pizza divided into 100 equal pieces: - 50% means 50 pieces out of 100 — half the pizza. - 25% means 25 pieces out of 100 — one-fourth. - 10% means 10 pieces out of 100. Important exam shortcuts: Percentage Easy method 50% Divide by 2 25% Divide by 4 10% Divide by 10 20% Divide by 5 5% Find 10%, then divide by 2 Questions 1. What is 25% of 360? A. 540 B. 600 C. 660 D. 720 Ratio and Proportion Batch 1 of 4 Questions 1–25 Quick lesson: What is a ratio? A ratio compares two or more quantities. Imagine you have 2 red balls and 3 blue balls. The ratio of red balls to blue balls is: \[ 2:3 \] That means for every 2 red balls, there are 3 blue balls. Remember these exam rules: - Simplify ratios just like fractions. - Keep the order of quantities correct. - In a proportion, cross-multiply to find the missing value. - When dividing a total according to a ratio, add the ratio parts first. A. 18 B. 20 C. 22 D. 24 Averages — Batch 1 Questions 1–25 Averages are common in CSC numerical reasoning questions. We'll start with basic averages, then gradually introduce missing numbers, ages, scores, and real-life word problems. Quick lesson before answering The average tells us the equal share of a group. \[ \text{Average}=\frac{\text{Sum of all values}}{\text{Number of values}} \] For example, if three students score 80, 85, and 90: \[ \frac{80+85+90}{3}=\frac{255}{3}=85 \] Think of it like sharing 255 candies equally among 3 people. Each person gets 85. Practice questions 1. Find the average of 12, 15, 18, and 25. A. 16.5 B. 18 C. 17.5 D. 19 2. The average of five numbers is 24. Four of the numbers are 18, 22, 25, and 30. What is the fifth number? A. 25 B. 24 C. 26 D. 28 3. A student gets an average score of 75 in 8 examinations. What is the total of all 8 scores? A. 550 B. 575 C. 590 D. 600 4. The average of six numbers is 14. Five numbers are 12, 15, 10, 18, and 20. What is the missing number? A. 8 B. 9 C. 10 D. 11 5. The average age of four friends is 20 years. Complete the sequence. 3, 10, 29, 66, 127, ___ A. 196 B. 200 C. 218 D. 222 Algebra — Batch 1 Questions 1–25 Quick lesson: What is algebra? Think of x as a mystery box 📦. It contains a number, and your job is to discover what's inside. Example: \[ x+5=12 \] To find x, subtract 5 from both sides: \[ x=12-5=7 \] The mystery number is 7! Practice questions 1. A. 28 B. 30 C. 32 D. 34 Simple Interest Batch 1 of 4 Quick lesson before the questions Simple interest is the extra money earned or paid when money is borrowed or invested. The main formula is: \[ I = \frac{P \times R \times T}{100} \] - I = Interest - P = Principal (starting money) - R = Rate (percentage per year) - T = Time (in years) Example: You deposit ₱1,000 at 5% simple interest for 2 years. \[ I=\frac{1000\times5\times2}{100}=₱100 \] The interest is ₱100. The total amount becomes ₱1,100. Practice questions Remember: If the time is given in months, convert it into years first. \[ T=\frac{\text{number of months}}{12} \] For example, 6 months = \(6/12=0.5\) year. Try answering the questions before revealing the answer key. 😎 Questions 1–25 1. A person deposits ₱2,000 in a bank at 5% simple interest per year. How much interest will be earned after 3 years? A. ₱40,800 B. ₱41,400 C. ₱42,000 D. ₱42,300 Speed, Distance, and Time Batch 1 of 4 Quick lesson Remember these three formulas: \[ D=S\times T \] \[ S=\frac{D}{T} \] \[ T=\frac{D}{S} \] Where: - D = Distance - S = Speed - T = Time Example: A car travels at 60 km/h for 3 hours. \[ D=60\times3=180\text{ km} \] The car travels 180 kilometers. Important conversion: - Hours to minutes: multiply by 60. - Minutes to hours: divide by 60. - m/s to km/h: multiply by 3.6. - km/h to m/s: divide by 3.6. Practice questions 1–25 1. A car travels at 60 km/h for 3 hours. How far does it travel? A. 150 km B. 180 km C. 200 km D. 210 km 2. A bus covers 240 kilometers in 4 hours. What is its average speed? A. 6 hours B. 7 hours C. 8 hours D. 9 hours Mixed Word Problems Batch 1 of 4 Quick exam strategy Mixed word problems test whether you can identify the correct operation from the situation. Clue words What to do Total, altogether Add Difference, how many more Subtract Each, groups of Multiply Shared equally, per person Divide Percent, discount Calculate percentage Average Total ÷ number of items Ratio Divide into proportional parts Exam tip: Don't rush into calculating. Identify the connection between the first pair, then apply the same relationship to the second pair. Questions 1. AUTHOR : NOVEL :: COMPOSER : A. Orchestra B. Symphony C. Instrument D. Conductor 2. OASIS : DESERT :: ISLAND : A. Mountain B. Forest C. Ocean D. River 3. SCALPEL : SURGEON :: GAVEL : A. Lawyer B. Judge C. Witness D. Clerk 4. CATERPILLAR : BUTTERFLY :: TADPOLE : A. Frog B. Fish C. Freeze D. Expand 10. DEMOCRACY : PEOPLE :: MONARCHY : A. Parliament B. Citizens C. Constitution D. King 11. COMPASS : DIRECTION :: CLOCK : ____ A. Distance B. Speed C. Time D. Schedule 12. Climax Symbolic Logic New subtopic Batch 1 · Questions 1–25 Moderate–Difficult Target: 100 questions across four batches. For this topic, students will practice: - Understanding logical symbols. - Translating statements into symbols. - Negation and logical operators. - Conditional statements. - Converse, inverse, and contrapositive. - Evaluating truth values. - Logical equivalence. Symbol guide for this batch: Symbol Meaning \(\land\) AND \(\lor\) OR (inclusive) \(\neg\) NOT \(\rightarrow\) IF...THEN \(\leftrightarrow\) IF AND ONLY IF Questions 1–25 1. Let \(p\) mean "It is raining" and \(q\) mean "The road is wet." Which expression represents: "If it is raining, then the road is wet"? A. \(p \land q\) B. \(p \rightarrow q\) C. \(q \rightarrow p\) D. \(p \lor q\) 2. Which expression represents the negation of \(p \land q\)? A. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) B. \(p \lor q\) C. \(\neg p \lor \neg q\) D. \(p \rightarrow q\) 3. If \(p\) is TRUE and \(q\) is FALSE, what is the truth value of \(p \land q\)? A. True B. False C. Both true and false D. Cannot be determined 4. Which statement is logically equivalent to \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(p \land q\) B. \(p \lor q\) C. \(\neg p \land q\) D. \(\neg p \lor q\) 5. If \(p\) means "A student studies" and \(q\) means "A student passes," what does \(\neg q \rightarrow \neg p\) represent? A. If a student studies, the student passes. B. If a student passes, the student studies. C. If a student does not pass, the student does not study. D. If a student does not study, the student does not pass. 6. What is the converse of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(q \rightarrow p\) B. \(\neg p \rightarrow \neg q\) C. \(\neg q \rightarrow \neg p\) D. \(p \leftrightarrow q\) 7. What is the contrapositive of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(q \rightarrow p\) B. \(\neg p \rightarrow \neg q\) C. \(p \land q\) D. \(\neg q \rightarrow \neg p\) 8. If \(p\) is FALSE and \(q\) is TRUE, what is the truth value of \(p \lor q\)? A. False B. True C. Cannot be determined D. Both false and true 9. Which expression means "Neither \(p\) nor \(q\) is true"? A. \(p \lor q\) B. \(p \land q\) C. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) D. \(\neg p \lor \neg q\) 10. If \(p\) is TRUE and \(q\) is FALSE, what is the truth value of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. True B. False C. Both true and false D. Cannot be determined 11. Which expression represents "Either the employee is punctual or the employee is productive, but not both"? A. \(p \lor q\) B. \(p \land q\) C. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) D. \((p \lor q) \land \neg(p \land q)\) 12. What is the inverse of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(q \rightarrow p\) B. \(\neg p \rightarrow \neg q\) C. \(\neg q \rightarrow \neg p\) D. \(p \leftrightarrow q\) 13. If \(p\) and \(q\) are both TRUE, what is the truth value of \(p \leftrightarrow q\)? A. True B. False C. Cannot be determined D. Neither true nor false 14. Which statement is equivalent to \(\neg(p \lor q)\)? A. \(\neg p \lor \neg q\) B. \(p \land q\) C. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) D. \(p \lor \neg q\) 15. If \(p\) is TRUE, \(q\) is FALSE, and \(r\) is TRUE, what is the truth value of \((p \land q) \lor r\)? A. False B. True C. Both true and false D. Cannot be determined 16. Which expression represents: "The applicant will be accepted only if the applicant meets the qualifications"? Let \(p\) = applicant is accepted; \(q\) = applicant meets the qualifications. A. \(q \rightarrow p\) B. \(p \lor q\) C. \(p \land q\) D. \(p \rightarrow q\) 17. If \(p\) is FALSE and \(q\) is FALSE, what is the truth value of \(p \leftrightarrow q\)? A. True B. False C. Cannot be determined D. Both true and false 18. Which statement represents \(p \rightarrow (q \land r)\)? A. If \(p\), then \(q\) or \(r\). B. If \(p\), then both \(q\) and \(r\). C. If \(q\) and \(r\), then \(p\). D. \(p\) is true only when \(q\) is false. 19. What is the negation of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(\neg p \rightarrow \neg q\) B. \(p \lor q\) C. \(p \land \neg q\) D. \(\neg p \land q\) 20. If \(p\) is TRUE, \(q\) is TRUE, and \(r\) is FALSE, what is the truth value of \((p \lor r) \land q\)? A. False B. True C. Cannot be determined D. Neither true nor false 21. Which expression means "If the machine is operating, then it has electricity and fuel"? Let \(p\) = machine is operating; \(q\) = machine has electricity; \(r\) = machine has fuel. A. \(p \rightarrow (q \land r)\) B. \((q \land r) \rightarrow p\) C. \(p \lor (q \land r)\) D. \(p \rightarrow (q \lor r)\) 22. Which expression is a tautology? A. \(p \land \neg p\) B. \(p \lor \neg p\) C. \(p \rightarrow \neg p\) D. \(p \leftrightarrow \neg p\) 23. If \(p\) is TRUE and \(q\) is FALSE, what is the truth value of \(\neg p \lor q\)? A. True B. Both true and false C. False D. Cannot be determined 24. Which expression represents "It is not true that both applicants are qualified"? Let \(p\) = first applicant is qualified; \(q\) = second applicant is qualified. A. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) B. \(p \lor q\) C. \(p \land q\) D. \(\neg(p \land q)\) 25. If \(p \rightarrow q\) is TRUE and \(p\) is TRUE, what must be the truth value of \(q\)? A. False B. Cannot be determined C. Both true and false D. True 26. If \(p\) is FALSE and \(q\) is TRUE, what is the truth value of \(\neg p \land q\)? A. False B. True C. Cannot be determined D. Both true and false 27. Which expression represents the statement: "The employee receives a promotion if and only if the employee meets the performance requirements"? A. \(p \rightarrow q\) B. \(q \rightarrow p\) C. \(p \leftrightarrow q\) D. \(p \lor q\) Let \(p\) = employee receives a promotion; \(q\) = employee meets performance requirements. 28. Which statement is logically equivalent to \(\neg(p \land q)\)? A. \(\neg p \lor \neg q\) B. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) C. \(p \lor q\) D. \(p \land \neg q\) 29. If \(p \rightarrow q\) is TRUE and \(q\) is FALSE, what must be the truth value of \(p\)? A. True B. Cannot be determined C. Both true and false D. False 30. Which of the following is the converse of the statement: "If the alarm rings, then the guard responds"? A. If the guard does not respond, the alarm does not ring. B. If the guard responds, then the alarm rings. C. If the alarm does not ring, the guard does not respond. D. The alarm rings if and only if the guard responds. 31. If \(p\) is TRUE, \(q\) is FALSE, and \(r\) is FALSE, what is the truth value of \(p \land (q \lor r)\)? A. True B. Cannot be determined C. False D. Both true and false 32. Which expression represents: "The applicant will not be hired unless the applicant passes the interview"? Let \(p\) = applicant is hired; \(q\) = applicant passes the interview. A. \(p \rightarrow q\) B. \(q \rightarrow p\) C. \(p \land q\) D. \(p \lor q\) 33. Which of the following is the contrapositive of: "If a number is divisible by 4, then it is even"? A. If a number is even, then it is divisible by 4. B. If a number is not divisible by 4, then it is not even. C. If a number is odd, then it is not divisible by 4. D. If a number is not even, then it is not divisible by 4. 34. What is the truth value of \(p \leftrightarrow q\) when \(p\) is TRUE and \(q\) is FALSE? A. True B. False C. Cannot be determined D. Both true and false 35. Which expression represents "At least one of the two conditions is true"? A. \(p \land q\) B. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) C. \(p \lor q\) D. \(p \leftrightarrow q\) 36. If \(p\) is TRUE and \(q\) is TRUE, what is the truth value of \((p \rightarrow q) \land \neg q\)? A. True B. False C. Cannot be determined D. Both true and false 37. Which expression is logically equivalent to \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(q \rightarrow p\) B. \(\neg p \land q\) C. \(p \land \neg q\) D. \(\neg q \rightarrow \neg p\) 38. If the statement "All applicants who pass the examination are qualified" is represented by \(p \rightarrow q\), what does \(p\) represent? A. An applicant is qualified. B. An applicant passes the examination. C. An applicant fails the examination. D. An applicant is disqualified. 39. Which of the following is the inverse of: "If the student attends class, then the student learns the lesson"? A. If the student learns the lesson, then the student attends class. B. If the student does not learn the lesson, then the student does not attend class. C. If the student does not attend class, then the student does not learn the lesson. D. If the student does not attend class, then the student learns the lesson. 40. If \(p\) is FALSE, \(q\) is FALSE, and \(r\) is TRUE, what is the truth value of \((p \lor q) \rightarrow r\)? A. False B. Cannot be determined C. True D. Both true and false 41. Which expression represents: "The system works only if electricity is available"? Let \(p\) = system works; \(q\) = electricity is available. A. \(q \rightarrow p\) B. \(p \rightarrow q\) C. \(p \leftrightarrow q\) D. \(p \lor q\) 42. What is the negation of the statement: "Every employee is punctual"? A. No employee is punctual. B. Every employee is not punctual. C. At least one employee is not punctual. D. Some employees are punctual. 43. Which logical expression is a contradiction? A. \(p \lor \neg p\) B. \(p \land \neg p\) C. \(p \rightarrow p\) D. \(p \leftrightarrow p\) 44. If \(p \rightarrow q\) and \(q \rightarrow r\) are both TRUE, what can be concluded? A. \(r \rightarrow p\) B. \(p \rightarrow r\) C. \(q \rightarrow p\) D. \(r \rightarrow q\) 45. Which expression represents: "The applicant is qualified, but the applicant did not submit the required documents"? Let \(p\) = applicant is qualified; \(q\) = applicant submits the documents. A. \(p \lor \neg q\) B. \(\neg p \land q\) C. \(p \land \neg q\) D. \(p \rightarrow \neg q\) 46. If \(p\) is TRUE, \(q\) is FALSE, and \(r\) is TRUE, what is the truth value of \((p \leftrightarrow r) \land \neg q\)? A. False B. True C. Cannot be determined D. Both true and false 47. Which statement is equivalent to \(\neg(p \rightarrow q)\)? A. \(\neg p \lor q\) B. \(p \lor q\) C. \(\neg p \land q\) D. \(p \land \neg q\) 48. A student says: "If I study, I will pass." The student studied but did not pass. Which conclusion follows? A. The original conditional statement is false. B. The original conditional statement is true. C. The student did not study. D. The conclusion cannot be evaluated. 49. Which expression represents: "Neither the manager nor the supervisor approved the proposal"? Let \(p\) = manager approved the proposal; \(q\) = supervisor approved the proposal. A. \(p \lor q\) B. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) C. \(p \land q\) D. \(\neg p \lor \neg q\) 50. If \(p \leftrightarrow q\) is TRUE and \(p\) is FALSE, what must be the truth value of \(q\)? A. True B. Cannot be determined C. False D. Both true and false 51. If \(p\) is TRUE, \(q\) is FALSE, and \(r\) is TRUE, what is the truth value of: \[ (p \rightarrow q)\lor(r\land p) \] A. True B. False C. Both true and false D. Cannot be determined 52. Which expression represents: "A person can enter the building only if they have an identification card and authorization"? Let \(p\) = person can enter, \(q\) = has an identification card, \(r\) = has authorization. A. \((q\land r)\rightarrow p\) B. \(p\rightarrow(q\land r)\) C. \(p\rightarrow(q\lor r)\) D. \(p\leftrightarrow(q\land r)\) 53. What is logically equivalent to: \[ (p\rightarrow q)\land(p\rightarrow r) \] A. \(p\rightarrow(q\lor r)\) B. \(p\land(q\land r)\) C. \(p\rightarrow(q\land r)\) D. \((p\lor q)\land r\) 54. If \(p\leftrightarrow q\) is TRUE and \(q\leftrightarrow r\) is TRUE, what must be true? A. \(p\) and \(r\) have the same truth value. B. \(p\) and \(r\) have opposite truth values. C. \(p\) must be TRUE. D. \(r\) must be FALSE. 55. Which statement is the negation of: "Some employees are hardworking and punctual." A. All employees are hardworking and punctual. B. No employees are hardworking. C. Some employees are neither hardworking nor punctual. D. Every employee fails to be both hardworking and punctual. 56. If \(p\) is FALSE, \(q\) is TRUE, and \(r\) is FALSE, determine the truth value of: \[ \neg(p\lor r)\leftrightarrow q \] A. False B. True C. Both true and false D. Cannot be determined 57. Which expression is equivalent to: \[ p\rightarrow(q\rightarrow r) \] A. \((p\land q)\rightarrow r\) B. \(p\rightarrow(q\land r)\) C. \((p\lor q)\rightarrow r\) D. \(p\land(q\rightarrow r)\) 58. A school announces: "Students may graduate if they complete all required subjects and pass the final assessment." Which expression correctly represents the statement? Let \(p\) = student graduates, \(q\) = completes all required subjects, \(r\) = passes final assessment. A. \(p\rightarrow(q\land r)\) B. \((q\land r)\rightarrow p\) C. \(p\rightarrow(q\lor r)\) D. \(q\rightarrow(p\land r)\) 59. Which argument form is valid? A. If \(p\rightarrow q\), and \(q\) is TRUE, therefore \(p\) is TRUE. B. If \(p\rightarrow q\), and \(p\) is FALSE, therefore \(q\) is FALSE. C. If \(p\rightarrow q\), and \(q\) is FALSE, therefore \(p\) is FALSE. D. If \(p\rightarrow q\), and \(p\) is TRUE, therefore \(q\) is FALSE. 60. What is the truth value of: \[ (p\land q)\rightarrow(p\lor q) \] A. False only when both are false. B. Always true. C. Always false. D. True only when both are true. 61. Which expression represents "It is false that either the manager approved the request or the director rejected it"? Let \(p\) = manager approved the request; \(q\) = director rejected it. A. \(\neg p\lor\neg q\) B. \(p\land q\) C. \(\neg(p\lor q)\) D. \(p\rightarrow q\) 62. If \(p\rightarrow q\) is FALSE, which must be true? A. \(p\) is FALSE and \(q\) is TRUE. B. \(p\) is TRUE and \(q\) is FALSE. C. Both are TRUE. D. Both are FALSE. 63. Which statement is logically equivalent to: \[ \neg(p\rightarrow q) \] A. \(\neg p\lor q\) B. \(p\lor\neg q\) C. \(\neg p\land q\) D. \(p\land\neg q\) 64. Consider the statements: - If the server is overloaded, the website becomes slow. - If the website becomes slow, users experience delays. Which conclusion follows? A. If users experience delays, the server is overloaded. B. If the server is overloaded, users experience delays. C. If the server is not overloaded, users experience no delays. D. If users experience delays, the website must be fast. 65. Which expression is a logical contradiction? A. \((p\lor q)\land(\neg p\land\neg q)\) B. \(p\lor\neg p\) C. \(p\rightarrow p\) D. \((p\land q)\rightarrow p\) 66. If \(p\) is TRUE, \(q\) is TRUE, and \(r\) is FALSE, what is the truth value of: \[ (p\leftrightarrow q)\rightarrow\neg r \] A. False B. True C. Both true and false D. Cannot be determined 67. Which expression represents "The applicant is qualified exactly when the applicant passes the examination"? A. \(p\rightarrow q\) B. \(q\rightarrow p\) C. \(p\lor q\) D. \(p\leftrightarrow q\) 68. What is the inverse of the statement: "If a number is divisible by 6, then it is divisible by 3"? A. If a number is not divisible by 6, then it is not divisible by 3. B. If a number is divisible by 3, then it is divisible by 6. C. If a number is not divisible by 6, then it is divisible by 3. D. If a number is not divisible by 3, then it is not divisible by 6. 69. Which expression is equivalent to: \[ (p\lor q)\land(p\lor r) \] A. \(p\lor(q\land r)\) B. \(p\land(q\lor r)\) C. \((p\land q)\lor r\) D. \(p\lor q\lor r\) 70. A company states: "An employee receives a bonus if and only if the employee meets the sales target or receives an outstanding performance rating." Which expression represents this statement? Let \(p\) = employee receives a bonus; \(q\) = meets sales target; \(r\) = receives outstanding rating. A. \(p\rightarrow(q\land r)\) B. \((q\lor r)\rightarrow p\) C. \(p\leftrightarrow(q\lor r)\) D. \(p\leftrightarrow(q\land r)\) 71. If \(p\rightarrow q\) is TRUE and \(q\rightarrow r\) is TRUE, which statement is necessarily TRUE? A. \(r\rightarrow p\) B. \(q\rightarrow p\) C. \(r\rightarrow q\) D. \(p\rightarrow r\) 72. Which expression is equivalent to: \[ \neg(p\leftrightarrow q) \] A. \((p\land q)\lor(\neg p\land\neg q)\) B. \((p\land\neg q)\lor(\neg p\land q)\) C. \((p\lor q)\land(\neg p\lor\neg q)\) D. \(p\land q\) 73. Consider the statements: - All licensed teachers are qualified. - Maria is a licensed teacher. What conclusion necessarily follows? A. Maria is qualified. B. Maria is not qualified. C. All qualified people are licensed teachers. D. Maria is the only qualified person. 74. Which expression represents: "Neither the machine is defective nor the operator is inexperienced"? Let \(p\) = machine is defective; \(q\) = operator is inexperienced. A. \(p\lor q\) B. \(\neg p\lor\neg q\) C. \(\neg p\land\neg q\) D. \(p\land q\) 75. If the following statements are true: \[ p\rightarrow q \] \[ q\rightarrow r \] \[ \neg r \] What must be concluded? A. \(p\) is TRUE and \(q\) is TRUE. B. \(p\) is FALSE and \(q\) is FALSE. C. \(p\) is TRUE and \(q\) is FALSE. D. \(p\) is FALSE and \(q\) is TRUE. 76. If \(p \rightarrow q\) is false, which must be true? A. \(p\) is false and \(q\) is false B. \(p\) is true and \(q\) is false C. \(p\) is false and \(q\) is true D. Both \(p\) and \(q\) are true 77. Which expression is logically equivalent to \(\neg(p \land q)\)? A. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) B. \(p \lor q\) C. \(\neg p \lor \neg q\) D. \(p \land \neg q\) 78. Consider the statements: - If an applicant passes the examination, the applicant qualifies for the next stage. - Maria does not qualify for the next stage. What can be logically concluded? A. Maria passed the examination. B. Maria did not pass the examination. C. Maria may automatically enter the next stage. D. The examination was canceled. 79. Which statement is the converse of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(\neg p \rightarrow \neg q\) B. \(\neg q \rightarrow \neg p\) C. \(p \land q\) D. \(q \rightarrow p\) 80. If \(p\) is true and \(q\) is false, what is the truth value of \((p \lor q) \land \neg q\)? A. True B. False C. Both true and false D. Cannot be determined 81. Which expression represents “Neither \(p\) nor \(q\) is true”? A. \(p \lor q\) B. \(p \land q\) C. \(\neg p \land \neg q\) D. \(\neg p \lor \neg q\) 82. Given \(p \rightarrow q\) and \(q \rightarrow r\), which conclusion follows? A. \(r \rightarrow p\) B. \(p \rightarrow r\) C. \(q \rightarrow p\) D. \(r \rightarrow q\) 83. Which expression is equivalent to \(p \leftrightarrow q\)? A. \((p \land q) \lor (\neg p \land \neg q)\) B. \((p \land \neg q) \lor (\neg p \land q)\) C. \(p \lor q\) D. \(\neg p \land q\) 84. If \(p\) is false and \(q\) is true, what is the truth value of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. False B. Cannot be determined C. Both true and false D. True 85. Consider the argument: - If the machine is defective, production stops. - Production has not stopped. What conclusion is valid? A. The machine is defective. B. The machine is not defective. C. Production will stop later. D. The machine is switched off. 86. Which statement is the inverse of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(q \rightarrow p\) B. \(\neg q \rightarrow \neg p\) C. \(\neg p \rightarrow \neg q\) D. \(p \leftrightarrow q\) 87. A. \(p \land \neg p\) B. \(p \lor \neg p\) C. \(p \rightarrow \neg p\) D. \(p \leftrightarrow \neg p\) 91. If \(p \rightarrow q\) is true and \(p\) is true, what must be the truth value of \(q\)? A. False B. Cannot be determined C. Both true and false D. True 92. Which expression represents “If \(p\), then not \(q\)”? Some registered voters are teachers. C. All citizens are registered voters. D. All registered voters are citizens. 94. Which expression is logically equivalent to \((p \rightarrow q)\)? A. \(\neg p \lor q\) B. \(p \lor \neg q\) C. \(p \land q\) D. \(\neg p \land q\) 95. If \(p\) is false and \(q\) is false, what is the truth value of \(p \leftrightarrow q\)? A. False B. True C. Cannot be determined D. Both true and false 96. Which argument form is an example of modus tollens? A. If \(p\), then \(q\); \(p\); therefore \(q\). B. If \(p\), then \(q\); \(q\); therefore \(p\). C. If \(p\), then \(q\); not \(q\); therefore not \(p\). D. If \(p\), then \(q\); not \(p\); therefore not \(q\). 97. Given \(p \lor q\) and \(\neg p\), which conclusion follows? A. \(\neg q\) B. \(p \land q\) C. \(p\) D. \(q\) 98. Which expression is the negation of \(p \rightarrow q\)? A. \(p \land \neg q\) B. \(\neg p \land q\) C. \(\neg p \lor q\) D. \(p \lor q\) 99. Watch out for choices that are possible but not necessary. Questions 1. A city plans to build more bicycle lanes to reduce traffic congestion. The plan assumes that: A. All residents own bicycles. B. Some commuters will use bicycles instead of cars. C. Bicycle lanes are cheaper than roads. D. Traffic congestion is caused only by private vehicles. 2. A school introduces free breakfast for students because it believes that students who eat breakfast perform better in class. The argument assumes that: A. All students prefer eating breakfast at school. B. Teachers will also receive free meals. C. Eating breakfast can help improve students' classroom performance. D. Students who skip breakfast never study. 3. A company decides to advertise its products on social media to increase sales. This decision assumes that: A. Potential customers use social media. B. All social media users will buy the product. C. Traditional advertising is no longer effective. D. The company has unlimited advertising funds. 4. The government encourages people to use public transportation to reduce air pollution. This assumes that: A. Public transportation is always faster than private vehicles. B. Everyone can afford a private vehicle. C. Air pollution has no other causes. D. Public transportation produces less pollution per passenger than private vehicles. 5. A manager believes that allowing employees to work flexible hours will increase productivity. The manager assumes that: A. Employees dislike fixed working schedules. B. Employees can manage their time responsibly. C. Every employee prefers working at night. D. Flexible schedules eliminate all workplace problems. 6. A restaurant installs an online ordering system to serve customers faster. The decision assumes that: A. Customers are willing and able to place orders online. B. Customers no longer want to eat inside restaurants. C. Online orders are always more profitable. D. The restaurant will stop accepting cash. 7. A school purchases additional computers because it wants students to develop better digital skills. The decision assumes that: A. Every student already knows how to use a computer. B. Computers can completely replace teachers. C. Students will have opportunities to use the computers for learning. D. Digital skills are more important than all other subjects. 8. A local government plants trees along major roads to help reduce extreme heat. This plan assumes that: A. Trees can provide shade and help cool surrounding areas. B. Trees can prevent all forms of pollution. C. Every resident will plant a tree at home. D. Road traffic will completely disappear. 9. A business lowers the price of its products to attract more buyers. The strategy assumes that: A. Competitors will immediately close their businesses. B. Customers always choose the cheapest product. C. The business can continue operating at the lower price. D. The product will become more expensive in the future. 10. A hospital extends its operating hours to accommodate more patients. This assumes that: A. All patients prefer nighttime appointments. B. There are enough staff and resources to serve patients during the extended hours. C. The hospital will no longer experience emergencies. D. Every patient requires the same medical treatment. 11. A teacher gives students practice exercises before an examination because she believes practice improves test performance. The assumption is that: A. Students will answer the exercises honestly. B. All examinations contain exactly the same questions. C. Practice exercises are more important than lessons. D. Students who practice never make mistakes. 12. A city installs additional streetlights to improve public safety at night. This assumes that: A. Better lighting can help people see their surroundings and identify possible dangers. B. Drawing Conclusions Batch 1 of 4 Questions 1–25 Instructions: Read each statement carefully. Choose the conclusion that logically follows from the information given. Do not assume facts that are not stated. Questions 1. All employees assigned to the records department must complete data privacy training. Maria is assigned to the records department. Which conclusion follows? A. Maria has completed all company training programs. B. Maria is a department supervisor. C. Maria must complete data privacy training. D. Maria is responsible for training all employees. 2. Every registered applicant received an examination permit. Carlo received an examination permit. Which conclusion is logically valid? A. Carlo is definitely a registered applicant. B. Carlo may be a registered applicant, but the information does not prove it. C. Carlo has already taken the examination. D. All people who received permits passed the examination. 3. No expired products are allowed to be displayed for sale. Several products in Store A are expired. Which conclusion follows? A. Store A must close immediately. B. All products in Store A are expired. C. Store A has no customers. D. The expired products must not be displayed for sale. 4. Some employees in a company are licensed engineers. All licensed engineers must follow professional ethical standards. Which conclusion follows? A. Some employees must follow professional ethical standards. B. All employees are licensed engineers. C. No employee follows professional standards. D. All licensed engineers work for the same company. 5. A report states that the number of road accidents decreased after the city introduced stricter traffic enforcement. However, the report does not identify other possible causes. Which conclusion is most reasonable? A. Stricter enforcement is the only reason accidents decreased. B. Traffic accidents will never increase again. C. The decrease occurred after stricter enforcement, but this alone does not prove enforcement caused it. D. The city should stop enforcing traffic regulations. 6. All applicants who passed the written examination were invited to the final interview. Liza was not invited to the final interview. Which conclusion follows? A. Liza failed the written examination. B. Liza did not pass the written examination. C. Liza refused to attend the interview. D. Liza was not qualified for any position. 7. A school surveyed 500 students about their preferred learning method. Three hundred students preferred face-to-face instruction. Which conclusion is supported? A. Every student in the school prefers face-to-face instruction. B. Online learning is ineffective. C. All students who prefer face-to-face instruction have higher grades. D. Most surveyed students preferred face-to-face instruction. 8. All public libraries in the municipality are open to residents. The Central Library is a public library in the municipality. Which conclusion follows? A. The Central Library is open to residents. B. The Central Library is the largest library in the municipality. C. All residents regularly visit the Central Library. D. The Central Library provides free internet access. 9. A company requires employees to submit weekly reports. Some employees failed to submit their reports this week. Which conclusion follows? A. All employees failed to submit their reports. B. The company will dismiss the employees who failed. C. At least some employees did not comply with the weekly reporting requirement. D. The company no longer requires weekly reports. 10. All buses operating on Route 5 undergo daily safety inspections. Bus 18 operates on Route 5. Which conclusion follows? A. Bus 18 is the safest bus in the city. B. Bus 18 must undergo daily safety inspections. C. Bus 18 never experiences mechanical problems. D. All buses in the city operate on Route 5. 11. A survey found that 70% of employees who attended a time-management seminar reported improved task organization. Which conclusion is supported? A. Every seminar participant became more productive. B. The seminar guarantees improved work performance. C. Employees who did not attend the seminar cannot organize tasks. D. Most surveyed participants reported improved task organization. 12. No student without an identification card is allowed to enter the examination room. Pedro entered the examination room. Which conclusion follows? A. Pedro had an identification card. B. Pedro passed the examination. C. Pedro was the examination supervisor. D. Pedro arrived before all other students. 13. Every successful project submitted by the engineering department received approval from the project committee. Project X was not approved by the committee. Which conclusion follows? A. Project X was submitted late. B. Project X was not successful under the stated approval rule. C. Project X was never submitted. D. The engineering department has no successful projects. 14. Some workers in a factory operate heavy machinery. All workers who operate heavy machinery must wear protective equipment. Which conclusion follows? A. All factory workers operate heavy machinery. B. No factory worker needs protective equipment. C. Some factory workers must wear protective equipment. D. All protective equipment is used only in factories. 15. A town recorded an increase in tourism after opening a new historical museum. No information was provided about changes in transportation, advertising, or other attractions. Which conclusion is most justified? A. The museum is the only reason tourism increased. B. The museum caused every tourist to visit the town. C. Tourism will continue increasing every year. D. Tourism increased after the museum opened, but other factors may have contributed. 16. All candidates who meet the minimum qualifications are allowed to take the practical examination. Ben was not allowed to take the practical examination. Which conclusion follows? A. Ben did not meet the minimum qualifications. B. Ben passed the written examination. C. Ben refused to take the practical examination. D. Ben was automatically disqualified from future applications. 17. A company has 120 employees. Sixty-five employees use public transportation to travel to work, while the remaining employees use other transportation methods. Which conclusion follows? A. All employees use public transportation. B. More than half of the employees use public transportation. C. Every employee owns a private vehicle. D. No employee walks to work. 18. All official documents must be reviewed before publication. Document A was published yesterday. Which conclusion follows? A. Document A was reviewed before publication. B. Document A contains no errors. C. Document A was written by the director. D. All reviewed documents are published immediately. 19. A study observed that students who regularly reviewed their lessons tended to obtain higher examination scores than students who rarely reviewed. Which conclusion is most reasonable? A. Reviewing lessons guarantees high examination scores. B. Students who rarely review always fail. C. Regular review is associated with higher examination scores in the observed students. D. Intelligence has no effect on examination performance. 20. No temporary employee is eligible for the company's permanent employee benefits. Ramon is a temporary employee. Which conclusion follows? A. Ramon will become a permanent employee. B. Ramon is not eligible for the company's permanent employee benefits. C. Ramon receives no salary. D. Ramon has never received employee benefits of any kind. 21. Every department that exceeded its annual budget was required to submit an explanation. The Finance Department exceeded its annual budget. Which conclusion follows? A. The Finance Department must submit an explanation. B. The Finance Department will lose its funding. C. Every department exceeded its annual budget. D. The Finance Department made an illegal purchase. 22. A school has 40 classrooms. Eight classrooms have been equipped with smart boards.
